# 780: Public Outrage (Two-in-one Chapter)

"Let us congratulate contestant Johan for successfully defending his 'Master Ball Tier' throne!"

After confirming the results, the host's passionate voice spread throughout the entire arena.

The venue erupted in the most enthusiastic cheers.

This match not only showcased tactical confrontation but also the heated clash between trainers, Pokémon, and opponents—absolutely worth the ticket price.

Teacher Johan was as powerful as always.

As for N, though defeated, he also received tremendous recognition and approval.

"Though he lost, I acknowledge N's strength is truly formidable. His tactical reserves and tactical 'literacy' are excellent—worthy of being Teacher Johan's student."

"Six to one? Seemingly lost, but actually won big! Being able to score against Teacher Johan—that's something only Elite Four level could achieve, right?"

"Making Teacher Johan truly realize this is Master tier, not baby cup!"

"Teacher Johan—seemingly won but actually lost!"

Seeing various online comments, Johan wanted to laugh.

What did they mean?

Really wanted him to treat Master tier like baby cup?

These people seemed like haters individually, but "cut them open" and they'd all be his fans.

Black-to-fan confirmed.

Better than fan-to-black anyway.

Putting away his phone, Johan looked at the Black and White Dragons before him, eyes narrowing with slightly dangerous light.

"You two..."

[We just came to watch.]

Zekrom nudged Reshiram with its wing, indicating it was right, wasn't it?

[Yes! Yes!] Reshiram nodded seriously.

What a "just watching."

As a Dragon of Truth, when had you become so untruthful?

He naturally understood Reshiram and Zekrom's thoughts.

Weren't they just worried he and N would get too heated, causing the "tragedy" of the ancient Unova kingdom from a thousand years ago?

Wrong.

Such tragedy wouldn't repeat—it would definitely become Johan's one-sided crushing of N.

After all, in strength, ability, and connections, Johan maintained overwhelming advantages in all aspects.

Actually, as the Dragon of Truth, Reshiram might be fine since only Johan had gained its recognition.

But Zekrom would be difficult.

Not even considering who was stronger between Johan and N, as the Dragon of Ideals, it had to make a choice first.

An absolutely difficult dilemma.

Fortunately, Johan and N's relationship was clearly a very friendly mentor-student friendship.

Also, Galar region's Eternatus had been beaten into submission by Johan and was currently receiving "modification" under Professor Magnolia.

Who knew when it would be released.

The Sword and Shield duo, Zacian and Zamazenta, also had friendly warrior relationships with Johan and wouldn't react much to Zekrom and Reshiram's arrival.

"King of Galar" Calyrex needn't be mentioned—one of its beloved steeds was in Gengar's hands, having an even better relationship with Johan.

So Zekrom and Reshiram's appearance, besides causing some commotion, didn't create any destructive impact.

Otherwise... their trip to Galar region would conservatively require at least two or three battles.

"Since you're already here..."

Under Reshiram and Zekrom's slightly expectant gazes, Johan grinned:

"Just go back early."

Reshiram & Zekrom: "?"

Johan couldn't help but smile. "Just kidding. Since you're here, stay for dinner."

"Roar—"

"Roar—"

Zekrom and Reshiram looked at each other and called out happily twice.

Zekrom winked at Reshiram.

See? Told you there was no problem.

Reshiram nodded satisfactorily but soon paused, looking confused.

Wait? Am I the Dragon of Truth or are you the Dragon of Truth?

How do you seem more truthful than me?

"Come on, try this special blend."

In the villa garden in Wyndon City, Reshiram and Zekrom looked expectantly at the food Johan brought, eyes glowing.

[Special blend?]

Reshiram and Zekrom looked delighted.

Look how polite Johan was—they'd come all this way and he specially prepared food. So honored!

"That's right, 'Groudon Special.'"

Groudon Special?

This name... so domineering?

"Zekrom, you try first." Johan placed the plate before Zekrom.

Zekrom was overjoyed.

Not far away, Flint elbowed Lucian quietly: "See? Teacher Johan's going to give someone a hard time, right? From my experience..."

Lucian quickly distanced himself from Flint.

You know yet still say it?

Though normal people couldn't hear from this distance... was Teacher Johan normal?

Don't drag me down with your death wish!

The next second—

"Roar—!!!"

Zekrom roared, mouth spewing lightning mixed with red flames, even its black cheeks flushing slightly.

Reshiram beside it immediately stared wide-eyed.

When did you secretly learn my moves?

[So spicy, so spicy, so spicy!]

Zekrom's forehead sweated as its head turned frantically, seeking water.

"Here." Johan brought out pre-prepared milk. "Water won't help with spice—have some 'Moomoo Milk,' spice lover."

Glug glug glug—

After several bottles of "Moomoo Milk," Zekrom finally recovered, collapsing weakly on the ground.

Only then did Reshiram realize this wasn't Zekrom learning its moves but being overwhelmed by spice.

What level of spice could make a Thunder Dragon breathe fire?

Seeing Johan's smiling approach, Reshiram instinctively stepped back, swallowing.

[Jo-Johan, I, I, I...]

I'm your most "loyal" supporter!

"You won't eat?" Johan raised an eyebrow with a meaningful smile.

[Can, can I not eat?]

Reshiram stammered the question.

"Of course." Johan said meaningfully: "Just don't regret it."

As soon as he finished speaking, Zekrom collapsed on the ground suddenly sprang up, excitedly howling skyward.

[Exhilarating! So exhilarating!]

Hm?

Reshiram was stunned.

Exhilarating?

Weren't you about to lose control of bodily functions from the spice?

Zekrom drooled—not from hunger, but genuinely from the spice.

But how to put it... after the spice passed, that feeling of inner satisfaction and pleasure was really great.

Moreover, Zekrom felt its power seemed to have subtle changes.

This benefit was greater than Energy Cubes!

Johan smiled, then said irritably: "Alright, eat up. I said it was special for you two—would I trick you? Though spicy, spice has its benefits and unique flavors."

What a joke.

Give someone a hard time?

Was Old Johan someone who did that?

Besides, Johan didn't think Zekrom's actions were wrong—otherwise, should N be left alone and isolated?

Moreover, last time having Flint test it wasn't for nothing.

After Johan's adjustments, though still very spicy, it was actually much better than before.

Last time Flint ate just a small bite and maintained "red-braised pig head" appearance for three days.

If not for his distinctive explosion hair, he probably couldn't even register for the "Pokémon World Championships."

"Roar—"

After eating, Reshiram also couldn't help roaring and breathing flames—its flames were much more vigorous than Zekrom's.

However, Reshiram's spice tolerance was considerably better than Zekrom's. In just a moment, plus two bottles of "Moomoo Milk," it recovered.

[Exhilarating! Truly exhilarating!]

As a Fire-type Pokémon, Reshiram seemed to gain even greater benefits from the spicy "Groudon Special."

Seeing this scene, other Pokémon, plus Cynthia, Steven, Lance, N and others, all eagerly wanted to try.

Soon the courtyard filled with continuous "hiss-huff" sounds.

But in this "harmonious" atmosphere, some discordant voices always appeared, like...

"Spicy! Spicy! Spicy! Water! Milk! Quick! Quick! Quick—"

Flint's face was completely red, lips slightly swollen, sweating profusely like an ant on a hot pan, searching for water and milk.

But several bottles of "Moomoo Milk" provided no relief.

Everyone was amazed.

Though they also found it spicy, after eating they just needed a few gulps of milk. Their spice level was obviously much lower than what Reshiram and Zekrom ate.

Why was Flint's reaction...

Could it be because Flint simply couldn't handle spice?

"Ku?"

Infernape looked confused, taking a spoon to scoop a small half-spoonful from Flint's bowl into its mouth.

The next second—

"Ku—"

Infernape's entire body blazed with surging flames, head flames shooting skyward, body tense with arms spread, spewing scorching flames skyward.

Spicy!

Johan nearby acted "belatedly aware," slapping his forehead.

"Oops, seems I forgot to add spice-reducing ingredients for Flint."

Everyone: "..."

"Lucian, where are my ingredients? Did you see them?" Johan asked casually.

Lucian immediately broke into cold sweat. "Here, here they are, Teacher Johan. Probably because I took them here, you forgot to add them for Flint."

Mentally chanting: sacrifice friend not self—don't contact me anymore, Flint.

Johan showed "sudden realization": "So they're here."

"Oops, mistake, mistake. Lifetime reputation ruined in one moment."

Johan quietly put away the little drawing Jirachi had given him.

As a Fire-type Pokémon, after eating spicy blend, flame temperature surged at least seven or eight percent short-term.

Flint, as a Fire-type Pokémon specialist "humanoid Pokémon," theoretically... should be fine, right?

As trainers, one must understand Pokémon conditions...

The "Master Replacement Battle" continued intensely.

In the "Master Replacement Battle" stage, not every "Master Ball Tier" contestant could only accept one challenge.

Of course, losing in "Master Replacement Battle" meant only accepting one challenge.

So far, those who'd accepted two or more challenges included... Master Mustard.

Yes, Mustard was still struggling.

Today, there were two battles Johan found quite interesting.

First... Drake stubbornly challenged Pryce!

Though Pryce was just a Gym Leader, as one of the strong contenders for early "Pokémon World Championships" titles, Pryce's strength was unquestionably powerful.

He was only a Gym Leader purely because Pryce had lost much competitive spirit.

Otherwise, forget Johto League Elite Four—whether Lance could sit in the Johto League Champion position would be highly questionable.

More importantly... a Dragon-type Elite Four challenging Ice-specialist Pryce—what difference was this from "suicide"?

How was this different from Lance provoking Lorelei?

But according to Drake, this was their older generation's "grudge." With this appropriate occasion, they'd completely end this "grudge" here—afterward would be the young people's era.

Later, Johan learned from Champion Alder that Drake and Pryce's grudge began when they fought over the same girl in their youth.

This ignited Johan's gossip heart.

He also felt this battle should happen!

They should fight properly!

What fun was always watching young people's battles?

Older generation battles would definitely be more "brutal."

Drake's strength was formidable. If he didn't want Champion responsibilities and numerous duties, Hoenn League's Champion could have been his long ago.

Ace Salamence after Mega Evolution definitely had near-legendary strength.

And Pryce... was even more exaggerated.

Though Johan knew this world's Pryce wasn't the "Masked Man" Pryce he knew—not the one who could capture Ho-Oh.

But there were still similarities.

Like abnormally exaggerated strength.

He'd rarely acted in recent years, so few knew his true capabilities.

From Johan's understanding of Pryce, even if he suddenly brought out legendary-level Swinub or Delibird in competition, Johan wouldn't be particularly surprised.

Regarding Ice-types, even Lorelei, the rising Ice-type Elite Four star, had some gap compared to Pryce.

Besides this "Dragon vs Ice" battle somewhat "replicating" Lance vs Lorelei, another battle also drew Johan's attention.

Janine challenging "Gibeon"!

Regarding Janine, Johan knew she was Team Rocket's highest executive, one of three top executives.

Reportedly, Johto League intended to invite Janine to serve as Elite Four.

Janine's strength was indeed sufficient for Elite Four—after all, no Kanto League Gym Leader was weak.

Just... Champion was "poached" from Kanto League, Elite Four also from Kanto League—Johto League might as well completely take over Kanto.

As for watching this battle, Janine was just a minor reason. The bigger reason was "Gibeon."

Or rather, the Explorers executive named Spinel that Johan knew.

He wanted to know what the opponent intended by using "Gibeon's" name to participate in the "Pokémon World Championships."

Attract more attention from Geeta?

But with what Explorers and Outstanding Inc. had done... wasn't this like lighting lamps in toilets?

The events in Zarude Forest and the two Explorers executives—Johan had given everything to Geeta.

Probably Paldea League was already secretly investigating Outstanding Inc.

Moreover, theoretically, with the opponent's strength, it should be very difficult to maintain footing in "Pokémon World Championships'" "Master Ball Tier" competition.

Only because in "Master Replacement Battle," challengers were mostly people like Bruno, N, and Drake with specific challenge targets, so they wouldn't challenge the seemingly easiest target "Gibeon."

Fortunately, Janine was different.

Janine was obviously a "practical" person like Johan.

Challenge whoever had better winning chances.

Thus this battle occurred.

For Johan, understanding Spinel's purpose meant watching this battle wouldn't be wrong.

Besides him, Geeta was equally attentive.

Actually, if Janine didn't challenge, Geeta already planned to have Green challenge him.

Right, Paldea League's ultimate worker Green, with "Ultra Ball Tier's" 21st place—the challengers' last position—entered "Master Replacement Battle."

He never expected to qualify.

Mainly following Paldea League's intention to fight several matches daily.

Because there were prize money.

After fighting enough, he stopped.

Unexpectedly, he qualified.

So Green didn't care about winning in "Master Replacement Battle"—he just wanted to finish quickly, then use the "Master Replacement Battle" break to go home and lie flat.

"Drake has sent out his ace Salamence!!"

"While Gym Leader Pryce sent out... Delibird!"

Salamence VS Delibird!

"Old partner." Drake smiled, gently patting Salamence's neck. In his weathered eyes flashed scenes they'd experienced together. "Tell them that as men of the sea, mere ice and snow can't affect us at all!"

"Roar—!!!"

"Mega Evolution—Salamence!"

In brilliant light, Salamence was enveloped in evolutionary light, roars shaking the arena.

"Drake and his Salamence have Mega Evolved!!" the host hoarsely shouted.

"Hey!"

Pryce in his wheelchair exchanged glances with Delibird.

Old opponents indeed.

But with the emergence of enhancement methods like Mega Evolution, Z-Moves, Dynamax, Drake's strength had advanced further.

"But we haven't fallen behind either."

An Ice-type "Z-Crystal" appeared in Pryce's hand.

"Looks like Gym Leader Pryce chose Z-Moves!"

Mega Evolution VS Z-Moves!

Compared to Dynamax and Terastallization, Mega Evolution and Z-Moves obviously tested bonds between trainers and Pokémon more.

The upper limits of changes brought by these enhancement methods could almost be said to be determined by trainer-Pokémon bonds.

So Mega Evolution and Z-Moves were obviously more suitable for older generation trainers.

Of course, Pryce wouldn't need "dance" poses like other trainers to use Z-Moves. As a top-tier existence among the older generation, Pryce could manage this slight "change."

"Salamence, Hyper Voice!"

"Delibird, Subzero Slammer!"

Boom!!!

Afternoon.

Wyndon Stadium, highest viewing area railing, Johan leaned against it.

"Long time no see, Boss Giovanni." Johan didn't even turn his head, knowing who arrived.

Giovanni in a suit, hearing "Boss," showed barely perceptible amusement.

That Johan acknowledged him as "Boss" was enough.

"Been busy lately," Giovanni said flatly.

Indeed busy.

With Rose's fall and Macro Cosmos reorganizing with Galar League under Leon's leadership, some "blank areas" inevitably remained.

Rainbow Rocket seized opportunities to "absorb" part of Macro Cosmos's remaining holdings.

Of course, through legal, compliant means, at most... slightly using some force.

So Giovanni's participation in this "Pokémon World Championships" wasn't entirely just for competition and battles—it also meant helping "Rainbow Rocket" establish reputation and presence in Galar region.

"Mm." Johan nodded thoughtfully.

Giovanni glanced at him. "You get dividends too."

Having already arranged to entrust Team Rocket and Rainbow Rocket to Johan, Johan couldn't really be said to have dividends—calling Johan half-owner wouldn't be excessive.

"No, I mean..."

Johan glanced down briefly then quickly looked back up. "I'm not an undercover agent either. Next time we don't need to meet at such high places, right?"

"Hm?"

Giovanni obviously didn't understand Johan's reference.

"It's starting." Johan said.

Both turned attention back to the arena.

Janine and "Gibeon's" battle had begun!

Janine displayed the calm, composure, methodical approach a ninja should have, plus occasional brilliant tactics.

The opposing "Gibeon" also appeared very confident, quite strategically controlling everything with ease.

But this made Johan and Giovanni unconsciously frown.

"Are these Pokémon really his?" Johan questioned.

"Gibeon's"—or rather Spinel's—Pokémon differed greatly from Johan's impressions. Their fighting style seemed pre-planned and trained, lacking flexible adaptability. They showed slight delayed reactions to Spinel's occasional commands.

These details were naturally invisible to ordinary people, or most trainers.

But at Johan and Giovanni's level, they could sense abnormalities from small details.

"Who did you say he was last time?" Giovanni asked.

"Spinel, using Outstanding Inc. President and Explorers Chief Gibeon's name. Purpose temporarily unknown. As for strength..."

Bang!!

As soon as he finished speaking, Spinel's Skeledirge suddenly erupted, spewing terrifying flames that engulfed Janine's Ariados, catching Janine and Ariados completely off-guard.

Afterward, each Pokémon Spinel summoned had a sense of... getting stronger and more violent as battles progressed.

Giovanni watched his capable subordinate Janine being handled this way, expression becoming serious: "His Pokémon aren't right! Either injected or drugged!"

Hm?

Johan instinctively replied: "We will definitely win."

Giovanni turned his head.

?

"Cough cough, I mean the tournament has inspection systems. If he dares use it, he's not worried about being detected." Johan coughed twice.

Giovanni frowned.

"Rakua elements?" he asked.

Johan had mentioned this before.

"Most likely."

"Treating 'Master tier' as his testing ground?"

"I'm afraid... this is just his surface purpose," Johan said somewhat hesitantly.

"Surface purpose?"

Giovanni thought briefly and understood Johan's meaning.

Wasn't this putting himself under League's noses?

Even if League currently couldn't detect mysterious "Rakua elements," they'd eventually manage it. Then...

Not just Spinel, but probably the Explorers behind him and Chief Gibeon would also struggle...

Hm?

Thinking this, Johan and Giovanni exchanged glances.

This guy deliberately entered the game?

"Showing his 'value' to others? Expressing loyalty? Or 'inviting wolves in'?"

Johan indicated he couldn't see clearly.

Giovanni couldn't help but sneer: "Wasting thoughts on petty cleverness—can't achieve great things."

Bang!!!

On field, Janine lost another Pokémon.

The score was... three to four, Janine at disadvantage!

Honestly, Janine had skills—at least ordinary Elite Four strength, reaching average level.

But for Janine, this rigid, rule-bound fighting style wasn't her specialty.

As a ninja, poison expert, assassination, stealth, wilderness combat were her strengths.

At least unrestricted, unlimited battles would let her display abilities.

In formal battles, Janine's strength was constrained.

If this were wilderness, no matter how strong Spinel was or how much "Rakua elements" he used, he probably couldn't last ten seconds against Janine.

But this was a formal arena with formal competition.

"Go, Iron Treads!" Spinel smiled, summoning his fourth Pokémon.

Ground and Steel-type Paradox Pokémon—Pokémon from the future.

Iron Treads' appearance immediately caused significant commotion.

"Is, is this Donphan?"

"Why is it completely covered in metal? And the color seems different..."

"What Pokémon is this? Iron Treads? Never seen it!"

Paradox Pokémon—even Paldea League was still secretly researching without public announcement, not registered in Pokédexes. Naturally spectators didn't recognize such Paradox Pokémon.

Not just spectators—most contestants didn't recognize Iron Treads either.

"Janine's in trouble," Johan said grimly.

Now it was real trouble.

The opponent's targeting was too obvious.

Iron Treads' typing was Ground and Steel, making it difficult for Poison-specialist Janine to handle.

Not completely unmanageable.

Even with "Corrosion" ability specifically for Steel-types, Salazzle and Glimmora would be tricky since both were 4x weak to Ground.

Especially on this kind of arena—more troublesome.

Moreover...

"Show your true form, Terastallize Iron Treads!" Spinel threw his Tera Orb.

Regular Steel-type wasn't enough—Tera Steel too!

Logically, contestants only learned who challenged them on match morning.

Spinel was overly prepared.

"It's over."

Giovanni glanced at Iron Treads then turned to leave.

Though the match wasn't finished, Janine indeed couldn't win.

Being extensively pre-targeted, plus "Rakua elements" and Terastallization—Spinel's preparation thoroughness was staggering.

Janine... could only say Spinel might need to be careful on his way home.

On arena, Janine lost, but off-arena, three Spinels combined probably couldn't last a second against Janine.

Most importantly... Johan sensed some anger from Boss Giovanni.

Tsk tsk.

Though unsure of Spinel's purpose, simultaneously angering Boss Giovanni, Janine, Geeta—both sides...

His ending definitely wouldn't be good.

Enough.

No need to think about this guy anymore—he wouldn't go far.

Moment of silence, can't spare more.

Finally, Pryce and Spinel both successfully defended their "Master Ball Tier" positions.

Older generation battles were fascinating.

But Spinel's summoning of Paradox Pokémon Iron Treads also caused considerable commotion.

Never-before-seen Pokémon, not even registered in Pokédexes.

Could such Pokémon be allowed in Master tier competition?

Additionally, some sharp-eyed people also sensed something wrong with Spinel's Pokémon, strongly demanding League investigation.

In fact, League immediately investigated post-match, but the result...

This matter also led to long meetings among tournament organizers, primarily Galar League and Paldea League.

Spinel using some unknown enhancement method was almost certain.

But... undetectable.

Considering impact, League couldn't announce this undetectable enhancement method situation.

Otherwise... it wouldn't just appear in "Pokémon World Championships" but potentially all tournaments worldwide, large and small.

This risk must be prevented!

After discussion, the final decision:

Temporarily allow Spinel while secretly monitoring to ensure he couldn't escape the venue.

Once he was defeated, immediately arrest him!

After figuring out "Rakua elements" and Spinel's enhancement methods, then announce this matter.

As for losing to Spinel... League Masters should have sufficient methods to handle such small-time villains.

Just relying on petty cleverness.

Use absolute strength against him!

Spinel had caused public outrage.

Right, send that, that, that—send the most "tormenting" guy!

Him!

# 781: I Am Johan!

After several days of exciting battles, the "Master Replacement Battle" officially concluded.

It had to be said that Champions from various regions were indeed skilled, and veteran trainers also demonstrated exceptional abilities. All sixteen "Master Ball Tier" contestants lived up to the "Master" title.

Sixteen "Master Ball Tier" contestants and sixteen challengers total.

Only a few ultimately succeeded in challenging and "advancing."

The first was naturally Steven, who defeated former Paldea Champion Peony and claimed victory, advancing to Master Ball Tier.

The second was somewhat surprising—Brandon from the Battle Frontier, who with his three Legendary Titans' invincible might, actually defeated the "Royal Masked Man" and successfully advanced!

The so-called "Royal Masked Man" naturally referred to Professor Kukui.

If people like Leon and Geeta, who simultaneously served as League Champions and League Presidents, were extremely busy, then Professor Kukui not only served as Alola League President and Champion but also held positions as Alola region's chief Pokémon Professor and Pokémon School teacher.

That was truly... Professor Burnet could barely see him anymore.

Thus, the under-prepared Professor Kukui ultimately fell short by one move and lost.

But Johan suspected he was quite happy—finally having some time during the "Pokémon World Championships" break to spend more quality time with Professor Burnet.

Professor Kukui's disguised "Royal Masked Man" was also the only Champion to lose in this "Master Replacement Battle."

But considering Alola region's special circumstances, everyone could accept this.

The third successful challenger was... incredibly, Flint!

This guy actually beat Raihan!

Perhaps because he'd spent time with Johan and learned quite a bit, Flint had actually grasped some "Sunny Day team" techniques.

When a hothead learns "martial arts," it's truly hard to stop.

Couldn't really call them techniques—just... using Sunny Day to counter "weather prince" Raihan's various weather tactics.

Finally, relying on Infernape's "death battle," he forcefully performed an absolute comeback, taking down three opponents in a row, including Raihan's ace Duraludon.

It had to be said, Raihan's strength was actually quite formidable. If not for Leon overshadowing him, Raihan would definitely be more than just a Gym Leader in any other region.

Even in Kalos, Alola, or Unova regions, Raihan had the qualifications and ability to compete for Champion.

As for Flint... he was typically someone who rose to meet strength—he could battle desperately against slightly weaker opponents and create life-or-death situations against powerful foes.

Even when facing Cynthia, if Flint and his Infernape went berserk, sweeping two or three opponents wasn't impossible.

Unfortunately, when facing Garchomp, that "mountain," it could steadily suppress them.

Too bad Duraludon had almost no advantage against Infernape.

This match exceeded most people's expectations.

In post-match interviews, Flint stated: "Thanks to Teacher Johan's 'special blend' cuisine, I felt full of power."

!!

Teacher Johan was thrust into trending topics again.

Johan, who hadn't topped trending lists for winning matches, was sent to #1 trending by Flint's single interview after his victory.

This made Johan once again lament how the world had changed and people's hearts weren't what they used to be.

This created a situation...

"I say, you all..."

Looking at the large group of people sitting around the dining table waiting for meals, Johan pressed his temples, feeling a headache.

"Johan lad, don't talk so much, bring out the food!" Master Mustard called out cheerfully.

Under Flint's "influence," Johan's "workload" had skyrocketed.

Just... he could barely understand carefree veterans like Mustard and Alder coming, but why had Elite Four Agatha and Pryce also arrived?

Were your personalities really the type to mooch meals?

Though they still had matches and he could forcibly accept that, but... Drake, Wallace, Caitlin, Bruno, Raihan, Tobias, N—you guys were already eliminated, yet still came to join this commotion?

Speaking of which, it was rather unfortunate.

Flint had beaten Raihan, but Wallace hadn't succeeded in his challenge.

Actually, Wallace's best opponent should have been Lorelei, but Wallace chose to challenge Alder and was ultimately "disciplined" by the old man.

However, this fit the "atmosphere" of this "Pokémon World Championships'" "Master Replacement Battle" stage.

Except for a few people, most challengers chose opponents based purely on "whim"—fighting whoever they wanted, as if they had no intention of advancing to "Master Ball Tier."

Actually, Johan had thought of another possibility...

Could Wallace have already guessed that he would pass the Champion position to Steven? And given Steven's personality and position, he'd probably step down within a year or two, making Wallace an excellent choice.

Was he already intentionally "acting"?

No, impossible.

Besides himself, no one else should have such deep thoughts.

Of course, the most "bold" was definitely Tobias.

Because this guy's opponent was actually Granny Agatha!

When Johan learned this information, he was stunned for three seconds, then silently gave Tobias a thumbs up.

Brave, so brave!

Probably listened to a song called "Courage."

"I promised Darkrai I'd bring it to eat Teacher Johan's cooking," Tobias said without embarrassment.

Current Tobias, in some sense, had cooperative relationships with both Johan and Boss Giovanni.

The Pokémon trading cards he promoted were already being implemented under Rainbow Rocket Group's "Rainbow Entertainment" subsidiary and receiving good response.

And Johan... had legitimately gained card printing qualifications.

Though there were surprises and unexpected results, the final sixteen "Master Ball Tier" spots were confirmed.

They were: Agatha, Lance, Johan, Cynthia, Alder, Diantha, Leon, Geeta, Pryce, Giovanni, Lorelei, Mustard, Gibeon (Spinel), Steven, Brandon, and Flint.

After today, tomorrow would be the official Master Tournament draw ceremony.

The draw was said to be completely random and public—just didn't know who would face whom.

Johan only hoped not to encounter Agatha, Lance, Cynthia, Leon, Geeta, Pryce, Giovanni...

Hm?

Seemed like there weren't many he wanted to face?

Sigh, Master Tournament opponents were all too strong. Not wanting to face any of them—could he forfeit?

Should he make an extra-spicy version and take out everyone present?

The next day.

Galar region's Wyndon Stadium once again gathered worldwide attention.

With the "Master Replacement Battle's" conclusion, the "Pokémon World Championships" officially entered the final Master Tournament phase.

Today, the first round Master Tournament draw would take place here.

The audience seats remained packed, and major streaming channels were equally crowded.

The host's voice remained as passionate and enthusiastic as ever.

Johan and others, plus many already-eliminated participants, waited in the contestant viewing area for draw results.

"Alright! Without further ado, let's proceed with everyone's most anticipated draw!"

On the big screen, sixteen contestant portraits flashed as countdown time reached zero under countless expectant gazes.

The next second, lightning-bolt battle symbols appeared on screen as groupings were announced one after another.

Spectators held their breath, watching continuously jumping portraits, finding themselves unable to control their gasps of surprise.

[Lance VS Lorelei]

[Cynthia VS Diantha]

[Leon VS Flint]

[Agatha VS Brandon]

[Pryce VS Steven]

[Geeta VS Alder]

[Giovanni VS Mustard...]

When Johan saw the first three matchups, he immediately wanted to shout "rigged!"

Lance and Lorelei? Cynthia and Diantha? Leon and Flint?

This definitely had inside manipulation—Johan wouldn't believe otherwise.

The organizers claimed "completely random"?

He'd interrogate Leon properly later.

But as he continued looking, Johan's heart began racing.

Setting aside Granny Agatha versus Brandon, the Pryce versus Steven battle—Ice vs Steel types—seemed to favor Steven, but that was Pryce after all.

But thinking again, that was Steven after all!

Then: Geeta VS Alder!

A true Champion versus Champion clash!

What Johan most anticipated was definitely Boss Giovanni VS Master Mustard.

Good heavens!

This was almost a battle between two genuine "legends," right?

Boss Giovanni's strength, Master Mustard's abilities...

Tsk.

Even Johan couldn't wait to see the Master Tournament battles—truly fascinating!

Wait!

Suddenly, Johan realized something.

One, two, three, four... seven. One group was missing—two people, one being himself, so his opponent was...

[Johan VS Gibeon!]

Sure enough, the final group featured his name, with his opponent clearly being Spinel!

Johan instinctively looked toward Spinel's position.

The opponent was also looking his way.

Spinel smiled warmly, bowing slightly toward Johan in a polite, respectful, humble manner.

Johan returned an even warmer smile.

Both simultaneously turned away, their smiles disappearing.

Damn it.

He'd just said Spinel had angered Giovanni and Geeta so he didn't need to worry, yet the Master Tournament's first round immediately matched him against Spinel?

His previously decent mood soured upon seeing this draw.

He'd rather fight Flint than Spinel.

Not that Spinel was particularly strong.

Among all sixteen contestants, Spinel was probably the weakest.

Even accounting for his prepared Pokémon and "Rakua elements," Spinel's ranking wouldn't be high.

But... toads don't bite, but they're disgusting!

Was this draw truly random or fake random?

If truly random, there were so many "grudge matches."

But if fake random, why not have Chairman Geeta take down Spinel?

Anyway, after seeing his opponent was Spinel... Johan's mood wasn't great.

"Ka-kaah~"

Gengar naturally sensed Johan's mood, eyes rolling mischievously.

"No misbehaving," Johan patted the purple fatty's head, eyes narrowing as he said softly: "We'll settle this on the battlefield."

Since it was disgusting, he'd give the opponent hell on the battlefield.

After the draw concluded came schedule arrangements.

Like the "Master Replacement Battle" stage, sixteen to eight meant eight total battles in full rotation format—definitely couldn't finish in one or two days.

Soon the match order and schedule were announced.

Day one, first match—

[Johan VS Gibeon]

He was first up.

Now Johan was certain the tournament was rigged—claiming "truly random" was complete deception.

"Gibeon"—no, Spinel—had made too many obvious preparations when challenged by Janine in the "Master Replacement Battle."

This time they'd scheduled his battle with Johan for day one's first match, giving Spinel minimal preparation and planning time.

Seemed like... the League also greatly resented people like Spinel who you disliked but couldn't do anything about.

But that was normal.

After Spinel's match with Janine ended, online comments about Spinel and negative remarks about organizers weren't few.

People like Spinel, relying on petty cleverness, minor tricks, and small-scale technology to cause trouble in "Master Tournament" events, definitely created significant negative impact.

Online support for Johan was already overwhelming.

Such one-sided support was rare.

But Spinel wasn't without supporters—just fewer vocal ones.

This phenomenon indicated... some people were indeed noticing Spinel's currently undetectable Pokémon enhancement methods.

One of Spinel's surface goals was already showing results.

Besides discussions about Johan versus Spinel, online talk about other groupings was abundant.

Lance and Lorelei's "destined duel," the tournament arrangement between Champion sisters Cynthia and Diantha, received the most attention among all eight battles.

Leon versus Flint was dubbed "avenging brothers."

The clash between veterans Agatha and Brandon, the "exchange" between old-generation Pryce and young-generation Steven.

Every match had considerable attention.

Every battle had supporters on both sides.

Except for feeling uncomfortable about facing Spinel, Johan was very excited about this "Pokémon World Championships" overall.

The usually lively villa garden was particularly quiet tonight.

Without anyone causing commotion, Johan felt somewhat unaccustomed.

But considering tomorrow was his battle, plus everyone needing time to prepare after learning their opponents.

Even Cynthia needed sufficient personal time for adjustments, arrangements, and preparations when facing Diantha.

Pokémon conditions, tactical arrangements, analyzing opponents' Pokémon and tactical habits, switching carried items, preparing enhancement methods, etc.

All needed advance preparation.

But for Johan... he didn't need excessive preparation.

Because his understanding of Spinel was limited, and based on the latter's battle with Janine, he'd probably make unpredictable adjustments.

"Fine then, at least not so tiring..."

In a recliner under the tree, Johan enjoyed the moonlight while contentedly sipping juice.

Gurgle gurgle—

Johan looked up while biting his straw: "Come out, it's late at night."

From dense tree canopy, a ninja-clad figure descended slowly on spider silk.

The visitor was Janine.

"Advisor," Janine respectfully addressed Johan by his Team Rocket title.

Johan grimaced.

What era was this, still calling people "sir"?

"Want some juice?" Johan took a green fruit juice from nearby.

Simultaneously waving: "Hanging upside down too long causes blood rushing to the head—come down first."

Janine paused.

"Yes!"

Landing silently without a sound before Johan.

In this nighttime setting, with Janine's outfit and secretive corner behavior, unknowing observers might think Johan was hiring an assassin.

"Sit." Johan turned to get a small stool, then found... Janine had already sat on the grass.

Johan: "..."

Fine.

"Late night visit to ask me to 'avenge' you tomorrow?" Johan handed over juice, asking jokingly.

Honestly, Johan and Janine's relationship was quite good.

Back in Ecruteak City, they'd interacted, with Janine providing considerable help in developing Gengar's Poison-type abilities.

They occasionally communicated online, mostly Johan asking about Poison-type Pokémon matters.

Just... recently Janine found it increasingly difficult to answer Johan's questions.

Like: How much would Gengar's poison strengthen after absorbing Eternatus's Poison-type energy?

After reading such questions, Janine was completely bewildered.

What was Eternatus?

Could normal humans really answer such questions?

Janine, wearing a mask with unreadable expressions, simply produced documents and a hand-drawn map from somewhere, placing them before Johan.

"This is?"

"Outstanding Inc.'s, 'Gibeon's' arrangements in Wyndon City, including prepared Pokémon."

Johan understood.

No wonder Spinel could make such thorough preparations against Janine.

So the massive Outstanding Inc. corporation and ancient Explorers organization were backing him.

Those Pokémon might lack top-tier strength, but with "Rakua elements" it was hard to say.

"Mm." Johan nodded thoughtfully.

Janine asked: "We have considerable personnel in Galar region now. Should we take this opportunity to..."

She made a sharp slashing gesture.

Take them out!

Johan quickly pressed down her hands, saying seriously: "What are you thinking? We're not gangsters now—no wait, we were never gangsters."

Under Janine's confused gaze, Johan coughed lightly.

"Anyway, no need. Don't dirty our hands for such things—we're a legitimate organization now, legitimate!"

Legitimate organization?

Then what was she in the League?

"Just watch them, don't let them escape."

Johan drained his juice with "glug glug" sounds, clapped his hands, and stood.

His expression and aura immediately changed, voice carrying determination:

"Even if he could conjure flowers, even if he brought one or multiple legendary Pokémon, I'd still pin him down and give him the treatment he deserves!"

Turning to look at the somewhat dazed Janine sitting on the ground, he smiled slightly:

"I am Johan, after all!"

For a moment, Janine seemed to see Giovanni—the shadow of Boss Giovanni in his youth, that absolute confidence, that overwhelming presence.

No.

Current Teacher Johan was even stronger than young Boss Giovanni!

If Teacher Johan inherited Team Rocket's leadership, she'd be the first to support him with both hands!

And would definitely become Teacher Johan's most loyal supporter!

Janine immediately stood, assuming ninja posture, standing straight as a solemn voice came from behind her mask:

"Yes!"

Johan dropped his previous serious expression, returning to his harmless smile.

"Don't be so formal. It's just a minor character—paying too much attention actually lowers our status."

Janine nodded.

Though she'd lost in competition, that was competition. In the wild—no, even in a house—Janine could ensure he wouldn't see the next day's sun.

"Um..."

Janine suddenly lowered her voice.

"Mm?"

"Teacher Johan, you know our ninja families are sometimes traditional. To maintain family continuation, arranged marriages are common practice..."

"So?"

"My younger sister is eighteen..."

A question mark appeared above Johan's head.

"If Miss Cynthia doesn't mind—mmph!"

Johan was shocked.

He quickly covered Janine's mouth, eyes scanning around.

Are you trying to die?!

Even if you're Janine, no matter how strong your stealth abilities, facing our little cabbage would be certain death!

Such words shouldn't be spoken.

People would die... not just Janine, but including them.

Didn't you see even Gengar had hidden, covering its ears pretending not to hear?

Even ghosts were afraid!

Johan released his hand from Janine's mouth, coughing lightly with righteous indignation:

"Give up, Janine. I only love Cynthia!"

Janine paused, quickly saying: "This subordinate was presumptuous."

Johan waved dismissively: "As punishment, go back and reflect. Copy the 'Rainbow Rocket Group Manual' thirty times. Go!"

He winked at Janine.

"Yes!"

After Janine left, the faint psychic fluctuations finally disappeared "indignantly."

Johan gently wiped his forehead, sighing in relief.

Close call...

Janine's sudden appearance was fine, but it inevitably drew attention, like... Caitlin sleeping cuddled with Cynthia.

He'd avoided "unwarranted disaster," but as for Janine...

She could only pray for herself.

However, Caitlin's recent psychic control had improved significantly—Janine hadn't even sensed it.

As one of three executives alongside Sabrina, Janine actually had her own methods for sensing psychic power.

Just this visit was somewhat hasty, plus Janine never thought she'd need to worry about psychic detection at Teacher Johan's residence, so she hadn't prepared for it.

"Tsk."

Johan rubbed his chin twice, deciding to distract himself.

"Might as well prepare for battle and aim for quick resolution."

# 782: Attack is the Best Defense!

"Ladies and gentlemen, welcome to the live 'Pokémon World Championships' main tournament!!"

The long-awaited Master Tournament officially began. Sixteen "Master Ball Tier" contestants would compete in elimination rounds to determine the top eight.

The top eight would be crowned with the title "Eight Masters" by the League!

So while championship hopes might be slim, the "Eight Masters" title... was still worth competing for.

Accompanied by passionate commentary, cheers from the audience echoed throughout Wyndon Stadium.

"Without further ado, let's welcome the contestants for the Master Tournament's first battle! They are—"

"From Hoenn region, Hoenn League Champion, holder of titles including Person of Truth, Heart Master, Tactical Master, and many others—our great Teacher Johan!"

Boom—

The arena immediately erupted in intense cheers.

Though previous "double elimination" and "Master Replacement Battle" excitement had been high, now in the main tournament, audience enthusiasm had clearly reached new heights.

Especially since this was the Master Tournament's first match.

"And—" The excited, flushed host took a deep breath to calm himself.

"Gibeon!"

Hm?

Standing in the contestant tunnel, Spinel heard the host introduce Teacher Johan with so many titles and words, wondering how he'd be introduced himself, but...

That's it?!

No titles, no achievements, not even mentioning his homeland?

Moreover, audience cheers were obviously much quieter.

Spinel was internally proud, so hearing this inevitably sparked some resentment.

Fine, fine, targeting me, are you?

But Spinel quickly suppressed his anger.

He considered himself very calm, skilled at thinking and planning—a strategic mastermind, not some hothead who acted rashly.

Don't get angry, don't get angry...

Once I make this "great" Teacher Johan you're talking about look foolish, you'll see how capable I am.

Besides... they said Gibeon, what does that have to do with me, Spinel?

He was competing just to achieve several goals.

Thinking this, Spinel's mood improved considerably. Following staff signals, he emerged from the contestant tunnel into the spotlight...

Across from him, Johan still wore his black fitted suit, smiling as he and Gengar waved to the audience.

"Feels like... Teacher Johan is a bit different today," Flint whispered quietly.

Hearing this, Lucian startled and quickly distanced himself, afraid Flint would say something shocking again and drag him down.

But this time Flint's words gained agreement.

Steven nodded: "Teacher is indeed a bit different today."

How different?

Several people cast puzzled glances, looking again at Teacher Johan on the field.

Seemed the same...

Both Flint and Steven couldn't quite articulate it.

Cynthia held little Jirachi, gently rubbing its soft, dough-like cheeks, and said:

"Mm, more killing intent."

"Killing intent?" Everyone was stunned.

Murderous aura?

Teacher Johan wanted to silence someone?

Who?

Flint?

Flint: "?"

"Probably the opponent's behavior made Teacher unhappy," N said.

Days ago, Spinel and Janine's battle had caused quite an online stir, with many suspecting Spinel's Pokémon used special enhancement methods.

Since the League gave no answers, audiences could only speculate.

Some felt "Gibeon" used improper methods, while others believed in presumption of innocence—perhaps "Gibeon" discovered some special, positive approach?

Anyway, it caused considerable commotion.

But seeing Johan's current reaction and state... Johan probably knew what methods the opponent used and confirmed they were unfriendly to Pokémon, otherwise Teacher Johan wouldn't have changed.

Those who knew Johan understood: if he normally appeared very angry, he probably wasn't truly furious, but if he smiled while showing coldness, then probably...

Flint had considerable authority on this matter.

"Yes," Cynthia confirmed N's speculation.

She and Johan were among the few who knew about "Rakua elements" and their harm.

Trading temporary strength boosts for accelerated Pokémon cell division and reduced lifespan was little different from murdering Pokémon.

Both contestants positioned themselves on opposite sides of the field.

"Teacher Johan, we meet again..." Spinel smiled, following "convention"—openings usually involved pleasantries or trash talk.

Johan smiled and nodded slightly at Spinel, then turned to the referee:

"Referee, we're both ready."

Spinel: "?"

"Ah? Oh, oh right, okay."

The referee had been leisurely waiting for both contestants to finish pleasantries but was suddenly stunned by Johan's words.

He immediately raised his flags.

"Please summon your Pokémon!"

Spinel suddenly felt breathless, like he had something stuck in his throat.

A shadow of irritation flashed across his seemingly gentle expression, anger passing through his eyes.

Really think being Champion means everyone has to call you teacher respectfully, that any random person or ghost can become a teacher?

Spinel flicked his wrist, producing a Poké Ball and throwing it.

"Go, Beheeyem!"

Beheeyem looked like several stones stacked together, a Psychic-type classified as the Cerebral Pokémon.

Supposedly from space, Beheeyem possessed very powerful psychic abilities. Communication between Beheeyem remained undecipherable by humans, like cosmic "code."

Moreover, Beheeyem excelled at manipulating others, even controlling opponents' brains and altering memories.

Simultaneously, Johan threw his Poké Ball.

"Tyranitar."

"Bangi!!"

Excited Tyranitar spread its arms and roared, voice shaking the entire arena.

Fine sand swirled up with "rustling" sounds, quickly forming a terrifying sandstorm enveloping the field.

"Contestant 'Gibeon's' first Pokémon is Beheeyem, while Teacher Johan's first Pokémon is—'Desert Tyrant' Tyranitar!!"

Seeing such domineering Tyranitar, audiences seemed emotionally stirred, shouting loudly.

Seeing both had summoned Pokémon, the referee's flags immediately fell as he announced:

"Battle begin!"

"Pokémon World Championships" main tournament first match: Johan VS Spinel!

"Beheeyem, Trick Room!" Spinel unhesitatingly seized the initiative.

For his prepared team, Trick Room was crucial, even though Tyranitar wasn't particularly fast, even though Tyranitar's sandstorm posed significant threat.

Spinel and his backing team had specifically researched Johan, his Pokémon, tactical styles, etc.

First confirmed point: among Johan's Pokémon, even including rarely-appearing Rotom and Oranguru, none were very slow.

Like Gengar, Ninetales, Dragonite, Ceruledge, Serperior, etc.—all were very fast, using speed and agility for advantage.

Even Tinkaton and Urshifu weren't slow, and relatively slowest Tyranitar was actually quite agile.

This was one reason Johan rarely seized initiative in most battles.

Facing an all-fast lineup, Trick Room became especially crucial.

Teacher Johan didn't seize initiative—once Trick Room opened, they'd gain some control.

As for Psychic-type facing Dark-type Tyranitar being severely disadvantaged... this could be minimized through Pokémon switching.

Clearly, though Johan disliked Spinel, his ability to gain Gibeon's attention, stand out among Outstanding Inc. researchers, and hold sufficient authority in the Explorers showed his capability and skill.

Hum—

Psychic fluctuations appeared around Beheeyem as transparent black barriers rapidly expanded from its center.

Trick Room!

But... just as Beheeyem's Trick Room opened, a ferocious beast had already appeared before it through the flying sandstorm!

"When?!" Spinel was stunned.

Beheeyem's green eyes showed some confusion.

So fast...

Wait!

Didn't Teacher Johan usually not seize initiative?

Didn't he say he preferred being passive?

The next second, Johan's indifferent voice came from across the battlefield:

"Tyranitar, Crunch!"

"Bangi!!"

Tyranitar roared ferociously, its vicious appearance embodying the "Desert Tyrant." Its evil maw wrapped in dark energy immediately bit toward Beheeyem.

Terrifying bite force from Tyranitar that could even gnaw stone slabs—this bite...

Bang!!!

A considerable explosion erupted.

In the shadows, they saw... a crater had appeared in the rock field, with Beheeyem lying motionless inside.

For a moment, the noisy arena fell silent. All spectators stared wide-eyed, mouths agape, wanting to say something but not knowing what.

Even the referee was stunned for three seconds before finally reacting.

He immediately waved the flag representing Spinel's side, announcing somewhat hoarsely:

"Be-Beheeyem, unable to battle!"

Whoosh—!!

The entire venue erupted.

Massive noise exploded.

"No way, that's it? One-shot?"

"One move, direct KO?!"

"Even with type advantage, this is too sudden! Completely unlike Teacher Johan's previous battle style..."

"Looks like Teacher Johan has urgent business today."

"See! See! I told you, what Master Tournament—it's all baby cup!"

Audiences never expected to see such clean one-shot elimination in the Master Tournament main stage.

Flint in the contestant viewing area shrank his neck.

His premonition was right—today's Teacher Johan was quite irritable.

Lucian couldn't help glancing at Flint.

Had to admit, Flint constantly dancing on death's edge yet "surviving" until now showed keen instincts for real danger.

Today Flint had said few wrong things and been very well-behaved, not giving little Jirachi any ammunition.

Flint: I'm reckless, not suicidal.

"This doesn't even give the opponent a chance to use those enhancement methods..." Steven said quietly.

Even he felt a bit scared of angry Teacher...

Spinel also stared blankly at the fallen Beheeyem, not expecting this one-shot elimination.

Teacher Johan completely ignored their team's previous analysis and methods.

Caught him completely off-guard.

Spinel took a deep breath.

The only fortunate thing was Beheeyem had successfully activated Trick Room before defeat.

After looking deeply at Johan, Spinel recalled Beheeyem.

Looking at the churning sandstorm and fierce Tyranitar, feeling Johan's changed tactics, he suddenly didn't know which Pokémon to send.

While Spinel hesitated...

"Tyranitar, Dragon Dance!"

Johan's voice came again.

Spinel: "?!"

According to battle rules, when an opponent's Pokémon was defeated, there was a fifteen-second period where Pokémon couldn't use moves, with a countdown displayed on screen.

Once fifteen seconds passed, no such restrictions existed.

This rule prevented battle rhythm interruption while adding urgency—adaptability was also required of trainers.

But Spinel, surprised by Johan's tactical change and Beheeyem's instant defeat, plus his own hesitation, considered too long...

Teacher Johan really wasn't showing any mercy today.

Of course, if switching Pokémon mid-battle, no such time restrictions existed.

The longer the switching time, the greater advantages opponents might gain.

Spinel increasingly felt things exceeding his control.

He immediately threw a Poké Ball.

"Go, Annihilape!"

The appearing Pokémon resembled Primeape but with gray-white fur, dark gray body, and red eyes.

Annihilape—Fighting and Ghost-type Rage Monkey Pokémon, indeed related to Primeape as its evolution!

When Primeape used Rage Fist twenty times, was completely ignited by inner fury, and awakened that rage, it evolved into Annihilape!

In Pokédex terms: when inner fury broke through physical limitations, Primeape could possibly evolve into Annihilape.

This evolution difficulty wasn't easier than Farfetch'd evolving into Sirfetch'd.

As Fighting and Ghost-type, its typing was quite excellent, more importantly having 4x advantage over Tyranitar.

Annihilape was indeed suitable for "Trick Room teams."

Purely by base stats, Annihilape's Speed was considerably higher than Tyranitar's.

But base stats only represented part—trainer cultivation direction was key to influencing Pokémon speed, strength, etc.

Additionally, Annihilape wore black metal-like restraints on its left wrist and both ankles—important factors limiting its speed.

"Puchaa!!"

Upon appearing, Annihilape's eyes turned red, fur floating upright, showing extreme fury.

Its entire body radiated eerie pressure.

"Annihilape..." Johan's eyes glinted.

Logically, Annihilape suited Trick Room teams, could serve as physical attacker, and Fighting-type had 4x advantage over Tyranitar.

So... why had Spinel hesitated so long before?

Johan suddenly realized, while Spinel, after summoning Annihilape, faced Tyranitar's nearly-finished Dragon Dance and commanded without hesitation:

"Annihilape, Rage Fist!"

"Puchaa!!"

Annihilape roared as if screaming, fists manifesting extremely dense Ghost-type energy like solid matter, wrapped in boundless fury.

"Using Ghost-type Rage Fist against Dark-type Tyranitar?"

"But... this punch seems unusually powerful..."

"And this Annihilape's condition..."

Rumble—

Annihilape's punch created continuous rumbling before even touching Tyranitar.

[Rage Fist: Converts anger into power. The more attacks received, the higher this move's power. Base power 50, maximum 350!]

Spinel's Annihilape's current Rage Fist...

Yes, no doubt—this was the 350-power move!

Because Rage Fist had another characteristic: its power wouldn't reset due to Pokémon switching or fainting!

As widely known, in Pokémon battles, most stat boosts and enhancements reset after switching—like Serperior's "Contrary" with Leaf Storm's Special Attack boost, or Tyranitar's Dragon Dance Speed and Attack boosts.

Once Pokémon were recalled, stats reset.

Hence moves like Baton Pass to transfer stats.

But Rage Fist was different.

Its power only related to how many attacks Annihilape had endured and fury accumulated.

And fury... wouldn't disappear from being switched out, or even from fainting.

Last night, Spinel had maximized Annihilape's fury—this Rage Fist was at maximum 350 power!

This was why Spinel had hesitated.

Beheeyem's sudden elimination disrupted his original plan and rhythm.

According to his arrangement, Annihilape's Rage Fist should have targeted Gengar, or at least Ceruledge!

Now forced to use it on Tyranitar, which resisted Ghost-type 2x.

However, Spinel felt that though not used on the ideal opponent, it should suffice.

Maximum-power Rage Fist plus "Rakua elements" not only enhanced Annihilape's abilities but amplified its fury.

Though this move was resisted 2x by Tyranitar... accounting for "Rakua elements" influence, it should at least deal normal damage!

Regarding Spinel's "schemes," after seeing his hesitation before sending Annihilape, Johan had roughly guessed.

Others stored electricity in balls—Spinel stored rage.

But... if you're playing dirty, don't blame us for being unreasonable.

"Bangi!!"

Surrounding sand continuously gathered toward Tyranitar, blocking Annihilape's path under Tyranitar's control, obstructing its vision while slightly hindering Rage Fist's advance.

Through commentary explanation, audiences finally understood why Annihilape used Ghost-type moves against Tyranitar.

They also grasped how exaggerated this punch's power would be.

"Damn, isn't pre-accumulating rage cheating?"

"Tournament rules don't have that clause..."

"Honestly, though sneaky, this is the Pokémon and move's inherent trait—hard to judge."

"I get it! I'll catch an Annihilape and play this way in competitions!"

"Catch one? You make it sound easy—are Annihilape that easy to catch?"

Primeape were notoriously difficult, with violent temperaments often uncontrollable even toward trainers.

Only martial artists with sufficient ability and charisma could make Primeape submit.

Let alone Primeape's evolution Annihilape.

"Annihilape" itself indicated many problems.

Moreover, having Annihilape pre-accumulate rage—could you ensure it wouldn't attack the trainer first before calming down?

"What about Teacher Johan and Tyranitar? Could it be..."

"Though sandstorm is large, it only blocks vision—sandstorm can't stop Rage Fist."

"Protect—can Protect block this punch?"

Protect also had defensive limits.

Soon, Johan provided the best "answer."

"Tyranitar!" Johan's eyes flickered with light blue as he shouted loudly.

Forcefully swinging his arm:

"Precipice Blades!"

"Bangi—!!"

Tyranitar roared skyward like Godzilla, lifting its foot and stomping heavily.

The next second—

Rumble—

The entire Wyndon Stadium field shook, temperature suddenly rising. The center cracked open massively as a molten lava-wrapped spike like red-hot iron erupted from the ground.

The venue exploded, livestream chat erupted, forums nearly crashed.

Precipice Blades!

Wasn't this Groudon's signature move?!

Angry Annihilape instinctively looked down as rumbling shook and the entire earth seemed to boil.

Suddenly, a sharp rock blade erupted beneath it. Annihilape's red eyes flashed with shock as the scorching blade struck its abdomen, shooting skyward in violent explosion!

Boom!!

Spinel looked up blankly, mouth agape.

Really, really Precipice Blades?!

Only because Wyndon Stadium was prepared for Dynamax battles did Precipice Blades not pierce the arena's energy barrier.

But even so... the field was completely shattered and chaotic.

The referee pressed against the arena's edge wall, trembling Aegislash held before him...

Johan's coat fluttered, expression stern.

Defense?

Our old Tyranitar never knew what defense meant!

Attack was the best defense!

Your Rage Fist still had to charge over—our Precipice Blades locked on directly!

----------

# 783: Paradox Pokémon!

"An-another one-shot?"

"If this move didn't one-shot, that would be unreasonable..."

Looking at the terrifying rock blade wrapped in fiery lava that nearly pierced through the entire massive Wyndon Stadium barrier, everyone couldn't help but feel their hearts tremble.

They dared not imagine what would happen if this move hit them or their Pokémon.

Moreover, the audience hadn't expected this.

They were still worrying about how Teacher Johan and Tyranitar would block Annihilape's Rage Fist, when Teacher Johan and Tyranitar demonstrated the simplest and most efficient solution.

Before the opponent's fist reached them, just knock the opponent away first!

What a "simple," direct, and clear solution...

However, after the shock, everyone could feel it now.

Teacher Johan seemed somewhat "irritable" today?

Showing absolutely no mercy in his attacks...

Two moves had already one-shotted two of his opponent's Pokémon.

"Annihilape, un-unable to battle!" The referee emerged from behind Aegislash, glanced at Annihilape still hanging in the sky without movement, swallowed, wiped sweat from his forehead, and announced the result.

At this moment, Spinel's expression had turned iron-blue after experiencing initial shock, bewilderment, and disbelief.

Even the audience could sense it—as his opponent, he naturally felt it more clearly.

Johan seemed to be targeting him specifically.

But carefully thinking back, Spinel felt he hadn't offended Johan at all?

He'd even maintained a very friendly attitude throughout.

Though Agate and Onyx were captured, after Geeta began being wary of Outstanding Inc., she'd actually kept that information internal.

Even with Outstanding Inc.'s infiltration of Paldea League, they only knew the two were captured but completely unaware of specific details.

So Spinel had no idea why Johan was targeting him.

They'd only met once before.

Could it be... because he'd spoken with Cynthia last time?

So petty?!

But regardless of Johan's thoughts, what he was doing had completely disrupted Spinel's arrangements and plans.

Only at the referee's signal did Spinel snap back to reality, taking out a Poké Ball to recall Annihilape.

This Pokémon he'd considered one of his trump cards hadn't even gotten a chance to hit the opponent...

However, seeing Tyranitar gasping heavily after unleashing the terrifying Precipice Blades, Spinel's mood improved slightly.

If Tyranitar could unleash such moves unlimited... there'd be no point fighting—who could withstand that?

Now it seemed barely acceptable, at least the Trick Room...

Hm?

Just thinking this, Spinel looked carefully at the field.

Where was any Trick Room?!

Tyranitar's Precipice Blades had not only taken out Annihilape but also directly shattered the Trick Room that Beheeyem had left behind.

This was possible too?

The audience also noticed this.

"The Trick Room is gone?!"

"This move can also destroy spatial moves like Trick Room?"

"You don't understand—Champion Cynthia's Garchomp has a famous scene of directly tearing apart Trick Room with Dragon Rush!"

"Champion Cynthia? Sister Cabbage? I see... no wonder... birds of a feather flock together."

Spinel's eye twitched wildly.

Not even pretending anymore.

Seeing the fifteen seconds almost up, Spinel had no time to "grieve" Annihilape's defeat and immediately threw another Poké Ball.

Taking advantage of Tyranitar's heavy stamina consumption!

"Go! Iron Treads!"

Again, that future Paradox species Iron Treads that had forced Janine into a difficult position.

Though seeing Iron Treads for the second time, audiences were still amazed by such a Pokémon.

Because even after all these days, Paldea League still hadn't released information about Iron Treads.

Not that they didn't want to—Paldea League was still researching Paradox Pokémon, so even if announced, available information would be limited.

More importantly, Paldea League still sealed the "Great Crater of Paldea" entrance, blocking passages to the depths, fearing awakening the terrifying existence within and causing irreversible destruction.

Once Paradox species information was fully public, masses of people flocking to the "Great Crater of Paldea" applying for deep access would become unmanageable.

Paldea League had actually reported this to the main League.

Involving past and future matters, the League dared not be careless.

As for where Spinel's Iron Treads came from... there were actually some Paradox Pokémon outside the "Great Crater of Paldea," just extremely rare.

Since their appearance resembled original Pokémon, even accidental sightings wouldn't cause much panic. The League would secretly monitor after discovering one.

This served both observation and research purposes while protecting traveling trainers from interference.

But Paradox Pokémon that seemed mysterious to most trainers, before Johan...

"Iron Treads, Electric Terrain!"

Without Trick Room, the not-slow Iron Treads had room to act.

As soon as Iron Treads appeared, Spinel immediately commanded, afraid that being slightly slower would prevent even using moves.

Sizzle—

Centered on Iron Treads, an electric field immediately spread as flocculent golden currents quickly extended across the field.

Having seen Iron Treads once before, though not well understood, its changes after deploying Electric Terrain were known, so audiences weren't surprised.

Moreover, Tyranitar had changed from original Ground and Rock-type to Rock and Dark-type, so Electric moves could now be effective.

Hum—

Electric currents swirled around Iron Treads as its already metallic-lustrous heavy body seemed even brighter, defense rising!

This was the effect of combining Electric Terrain with future-species Paradox Pokémon abilities.

Future-species Paradox Pokémon had "Quark Drive" ability—on Electric Terrain, their most outstanding stat received significant enhancement.

If that Pokémon's most outstanding stat was Speed, the Speed boost would be even greater.

Johan was completely clear about this.

Future-species Paradox Pokémon needed pairing with Miraidon's "Hadron Engine" for maximum effect.

At minimum, they needed Tapu Koko.

Oh, the opponent didn't have those—then at absolute minimum, Pincurchin?

If none of those...

"Tyranitar!" Johan's mouth curved in a somewhat mocking arc. "Earthquake!"

Our Dragon Dance speed boost wasn't for show.

"Bangi!"

Tyranitar immediately dropped its previous gasping, exhausted appearance as cunning flashed in its fierce eyes.

I'm pretty smart too!

After mixing with the mischievous Gengar so long, how could Tyranitar deliberately show its weakest side to opponents?

Tyranitar's expression change was deliberately magnified on screens and livestreams. After initial bewilderment, audiences laughed and cursed.

Indeed! Not one of Teacher Johan's Pokémon was simple!

Even deceiving like this?

Rumble—

The already devastated field immediately shook violently as countless rocks and ground debris flew up. Terrifying earthquake shockwaves like wave crests rolled toward Iron Treads with Tyranitar at center.

The Electric Terrain Iron Treads deployed even slowed its spread under earthquake influence.

Boom!!

When earthquake ripples converged on Iron Treads from all directions, massive rumbling erupted as the arena ground suffered incredibly heavy impact again, letting out final wails.

Spinel hadn't expected Tyranitar's exhausted appearance was an act?!

How could this person be "dirty" to such an extent?

So compared to game Pokémon battles, Johan still preferred real-world Pokémon battles.

How wonderful—you could even play simple psychology with opponents, and they'd actually fall for it.

"Worthy of Teacher Johan, master of Pokémon psychology, this manipulation (snapping fingers)."

"Did the opponent really research Teacher Johan? Not even knowing Teacher Johan's most 'basic' psychological tactics?"

"What videos could he research Teacher Johan with? Baby cup battle videos?"

However, Tyranitar's Earthquake didn't one-shot Iron Treads, even though Iron Treads was 2x weak to Ground.

Partly because Iron Treads' physical defense was indeed significantly strengthened under "Quark Drive" and Electric Terrain.

Moreover, there was "Rakua element" enhancement.

Spinel's Pokémon were affected differently by "Rakua elements."

Like the previous Annihilape, already explosive by nature, was less affected by "Rakua elements"—otherwise it would have beaten up Spinel before even entering battle.

But Iron Treads was more heavily affected, probably because mechanized future species had stronger tolerance.

On the other hand, though Tyranitar was acting, using Precipice Blades indeed consumed considerable energy—after all, this seemed to be Tyranitar's first time using Precipice Blades in non-Mega Evolution form.

This factor was more critical than the former.

But it was enough.

When Iron Treads finally struggled out of earthquake debris, it saw...

A fierce-faced figure suddenly appeared before it, grinning with an expression of "oh, you're finally out."

Then, under Iron Treads' widening eyes, that thick arm swung out, wrapped in brutal Ground-type energy, pressing tightly against Iron Treads' face.

Bulldoze!

The next second, the mechanized, heavy-looking Iron Treads flew like a cannonball across the field, grazing Spinel's face before heavily crashing into the arena barrier behind him.

Rumble—

The arena energy barrier shook violently as wave after wave of ripples spread.

Spinel stared blankly, a drop of cold sweat forming on his forehead.

If Tyranitar's slap had been slightly more "accurate," he'd be done for, right?

"I-Iron Treads, unable to battle!" The referee's current thoughts matched Spinel's exactly.

Thud—

On the big screen next to Spinel's portrait, the icons of Beheeyem, Annihilape, and Iron Treads had dimmed.

More than half lost!

Audiences snapped back to reality.

"Damn! One-shot, one-shot, two-shot—is this really Master tier?"

"Am I witnessing the fastest 'Master Tournament' speedrun record?"

"This isn't the vehicle to 'Master Tournament'—this is the vehicle to 'kindergarten'! Quick! I want off!"

Exaggerated!

Outrageous!

Teacher Johan's tactics today were truly brutal.

But... it looked so satisfying!

"Halftime, field change!" The referee reacted and quickly announced.

The current rock field could no longer be described as dilapidated—it felt like it would completely collapse if Tyranitar wreaked havoc a bit more.

Field change!

According to pre-match draws, Johan and Spinel's battle had rock field for the first half and forest field for the second half.

The field change gave Spinel more thinking time.

Johan's crushing performance this match had truly shocked Spinel considerably.

No matter how much preparation he'd done beforehand, once on the battlefield, he felt... like Johan had stunned him with three slaps.

Soon, field change completed.

"Contestant 'Gibeon'? Contestant 'Gibeon'?" Seeing Spinel still dazed, the referee called out.

Spinel snapped back to reality.

He heard surrounding audience whispers like... "Could he have been beaten silly by Teacher Johan? That won't do." "Hey, brother, losing to Teacher Johan isn't shameful..."

Such words deeply wounded Spinel's extremely strong pride.

He'd long lost his previous gentle, friendly appearance, brows full of gloom.

After glaring viciously at Johan and Tyranitar, Spinel threw his fourth Poké Ball.

Though Iron Treads was defeated, he'd truly confirmed Tyranitar's condition this time.

At least... at least he couldn't be one-shotted again, right? At least he should defeat one of Johan's Pokémon?

"Flutter Mane!" Spinel's voice carried some hoarseness and depth.

What appeared was a Pokémon resembling Misdreavus but looking wilder and more violent.

Sister Mane?

Seeing Flutter Mane, Johan raised an eyebrow with some surprise.

If asked which Paldea region Paradox Pokémon he was most familiar with, Flutter Mane "Sister Mane" would definitely be in the top three.

Flutter Mane—Ghost and Fairy-type Paradox Pokémon.

Unlike Iron Treads, though also a Paradox Pokémon, Flutter Mane was an ancient species.

Its ability wasn't "Quark Drive" but "Protosynthesis."

You even have "Sister Mane"... what were you doing with Beheeyem opening Trick Room at the start?

Playing me?

Johan narrowed his eyes, dangerous light flickering.

If Spinel knew Johan's thoughts, he'd definitely cry injustice.

Flutter Mane was fast, but that depended on comparison—compared to whose Pokémon!

Ghost and Fairy-type Flutter Mane facing Teacher Johan's Dragonite and Gengar—Flutter Mane's speed wouldn't be enough either, still needing Trick Room!

If Johan knew Spinel's pre-match thoughts, he'd probably laugh out loud.

Gengar?

You need our ghost to handle you?

I'm afraid Gengar would look down on me!

Of course, Johan knowing Flutter Mane didn't mean the audience recognized it.

Another "unknown" Pokémon similar to but different from others!

Both live and livestream audiences became noisy again.

Just using Pokémon others don't recognize?

Actually, seeing Flutter Mane made Johan want to say something to Geeta.

Didn't your Paldea League strictly control the "Great Crater of Paldea"?

How could Explorers' Spinel still capture so many Paradox Pokémon?

Were you sidelined, or disconnected from the people?

"Battle continue!" The referee waved flags.

"Flutter Mane!" Spinel shouted somewhat shrilly.

"Come back, Tyranitar." Johan took out a Poké Ball and recalled Tyranitar.

Spinel: "?"

His intended command suddenly stuck in his throat.

No, just recalled like that?

Johan's Pokémon switching speed was much faster than Spinel's.

Tyranitar's consumption was still significant—go back and munch some diamonds. Leave the rest to other partners.

"Time to work, Tinkaton!" Johan threw his Poké Ball.

"Flutter Mane, Sunny Day!"

Spinel quickly seized the last bit of time, not wasting this opportunity of Johan switching Pokémon.

With Tyranitar off the field, he could regain weather control.

Hum—

Sand dust gradually cleared as the sun above became scorching.

Simultaneously, Flutter Mane's "Protosynthesis" ability triggered, aura rising as Special Attack received significant enhancement.

"Quark Drive" relied on Electric Terrain while "Protosynthesis" relied on Sunny Day.

"Cha!"

Hammer-wielding Tinkaton frowned.

Strengthening right upon entering?

"Fake Out." Johan's voice came.

"Cha!"

Tinkaton swung its pink braids, grinned, and its seemingly slender arms suddenly exerted force, hurling the shoulder hammer.

Whoosh—

The heavy hammer broke through air.

"Flutter Mane, hide behind trees!" Spinel immediately waved and shouted.

Fake Out was a priority move, very fast. Flutter Mane having just used Sunny Day obviously couldn't use other moves in time.

Fortunately, the forest field had nothing else but plenty of trees.

But... ordinary trees before Hammer Sister's hammer were really...

Bang bang bang—

Smashing through numerous trees along the way, it still solidly hammered Flutter Mane.

It uncontrollably entered "flinch" status.

Johan raised his hand, speaking softly:

"Gigaton Hammer!"

"Cha!!"

The hammer had just hit Flutter Mane when the rebounding hammer was caught by closely following Tinkaton.

The incredibly heavy hammer seemed very light in Hammer Sister's hands.

Like drawing a bow, her body resembled a full bow. Before Flutter Mane recovered from "flinch" status, it heavily smashed down.

Bang!!

The floating Flutter Mane's body seemed flattened, then crashed into the ground like a cannonball.

Boom!!

After brief silence, the freshly changed field let out violent "wails" as the entire ground began collapsing with Flutter Mane at center.

The referee gaped.

The carefully changed field!

Spinel silently stood aside, watching Flutter Mane lying quietly in the debris crater.

Speechless.

He felt that after experiencing surprise, shock, anger, and burning rage, he'd become numb.

Two moves.

Two moves again.

One move was even Fake Out to limit Flutter Mane's actions...

Flutter Mane had just used Sunny Day, just triggered "Protosynthesis"!

Not just Spinel—even audiences seemed somewhat adapted.

Compared to this, audiences were more curious whether this battle could break the fastest "Master Tournament" completion record.

They were witnessing history!

The live audience's comments once again deeply wounded Spinel.

He expressionlessly recalled Flutter Mane.

Head down.

Forehead veins throbbing.

Honestly, Spinel didn't think he could beat Johan either.

This was a Champion after all!

If he could beat Johan, would he still need to remain under Gibeon? Would Explorers still need to carefully hide from Geeta and Paldea League?

He just hadn't expected the battle to reach this situation.

Completely different from battling Janine.

Were Champions really this strong?

Spinel's preference for petty cleverness was exactly why—such cleverness was worthless before absolute power gaps.

Comparing Janine to Johan?

In the wild, Janine might threaten Johan somewhat, but in formal battles without Johan holding back, Janine's result wouldn't differ much from Spinel's.

What was Tinkaton's strength?

After further strengthening its hammer in the Eternatus incident, Tinkaton had reached Champion peak level!

If Tinkaton faced Dynamax opponents... its displayed combat power would be considerably more formidable than normal.

And if facing Dynamax flying-type Pokémon... congratulations.

Tinkaton could even temporarily unleash legendary-level strength.

With such strength plus type advantage, if Gigaton Hammer couldn't defeat 2x Steel-weak Flutter Mane, that would be strange.

What next?

Already 0-4... might soon become 0-6, possibly the fastest 0-6 in Master Tournament history.

About to be nailed to the pillar of shame?

Spinel suddenly startled awake.

No!

He could lose.

But his pride and self-respect wouldn't allow losing this way.

Gritting his teeth, Spinel showed vicious, crazed expression.

"Then try this one!"

Red light flashed.

When live audiences saw the Pokémon Spinel summoned, considerable commotion immediately erupted.

"Entei?!"
